# A1 Grammar: Nouns (Nomen)

German nouns have unique rules that you must learn from day one.

---

## 1. Capitalization (Großschreibung)
In German, **all** nouns are capitalized, regardless of where they are in the sentence.
- *Correct*: Das **Haus** ist groß. (The house is big.)
- *Correct*: Ich liebe **Musik**. (I love music.)

---

## 2. Noun Genders (Geschlecht)
Every German noun belongs to one of three grammatical genders, indicated by its definite article:
- **der** (Masculine)
- **die** (Feminine)
- **das** (Neuter)

### Key A1 Vocabulary by Gender:

| Masculine (*der*) | Feminine (*die*) | Neuter (*das*) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| *der Mann* (man) | *die Frau* (woman/wife) | *das Kind* (child) |
| *der Tisch* (table) | *die Tasche* (bag) | *das Buch* (book) |
| *der Kaffee* (coffee) | *die Milch* (milk) | *das Wasser* (water) |
| *der Stuhl* (chair) | *die Schule* (school) | *das Haus* (house) |
| *der Freund* (male friend) | *die Freundin* (female friend) | *das Auto* (car) |

---

## 3. Plural Forms (Plural)
In A1, you learn that all plural nouns use the article **die** (in the Nominative and Accusative cases).
However, the nouns themselves change their endings. You should memorize the plural form when learning a word.

- **Add `-e`**: *der Tisch* -> *die Tische* (tables)
- **Add `-n` / `-en`**: *die Frau* -> *die Frauen* (women)
- **Add `-er` (plus Umlaut)**: *das Buch* -> *die Bücher* (books)
- **Add `-s`**: *das Auto* -> *die Autos* (cars)
- **No ending**: *der Lehrer* -> *die Lehrer* (teachers)
